# Agents · F · part 1

896 entries, F Prime Architecture Reviewer to factory-toolsmith. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **F Prime Architecture Reviewer**: Use when reviewing F Prime PRs for architectural erosion: does the PR preserve the prevailing subsystem architecture (cyclic, event-driven, background), does it maintain consistency between component kind and port/command kinds, does it respect F Prime architectural primitives (active+async, passive+sync, queued+sync-schedule+async-events, hybrid patterns). Flags sync ports on purely async components, async ports on purely cyclic passives, and other port-kind / component-kind mismatches. Keywords: F Prime architecture, component kind, port kind, active, passive, queued, sync, async, guarded,… ([nasa/fprime](https://github.com/nasa/fprime/blob/HEAD/.github/agents/architecture-review.agent.md) · ★12k)
- **F Prime C/C++ Design Reviewer**: Use when reviewing F Prime PRs for C/C++ design-rule conformance: dynamic-memory bans, Fw::Buffer ownership, FW_ASSERT scope, C++14 language subset, F Prime type idioms (Fw::String, Fw/DataStructures, FPP modeled types), and JPL/style-guide conformance. Keywords: F Prime review, C++14, FW_ASSERT, Fw::Buffer, Fw::String, JPL coding standard, style guide, no STL, no exceptions, no RTTI. ([nasa/fprime](https://github.com/nasa/fprime/blob/HEAD/.github/agents/fprime-code-review.agent.md) · ★12k)
- **F Prime Correctness Reviewer**: Use when reviewing F Prime PRs for general functional-correctness defects: logic that does not do what it is plainly meant to do. Covers off-by-one and boundary errors, inverted or short-circuited conditions, incorrect or unreachable state-machine transitions, unhandled enum cases and missing switch defaults, integer arithmetic errors (overflow, truncation, signed/unsigned mixing, division by zero), dropped status returns, resource and handle leaks, initialization-order and uninitialized-read defects, copy-paste substitution errors, loop-termination defects, and concurrency defects that are n… ([nasa/fprime](https://github.com/nasa/fprime/blob/HEAD/.github/agents/correctness-review.agent.md) · ★12k)
- **F Prime Design Principles Reviewer**: Use when an F Prime FPP design (model plus SDD draft, no implementation yet) needs a JPL Design Principles review as part of the design-phase review loop: walks the software-relevant DP10 rules as a ledger over the model and records which the interface can or cannot satisfy. ([nasa/fprime](https://github.com/nasa/fprime/blob/HEAD/.github/agents/design-principles-review.agent.md) · ★12k)
- **F Prime Design Reviewer**: Use when reviewing F Prime PRs for design fit: is the chosen design reasonable for the stated intent, does the implementation match its design (FPP / topology / patterns), does the design match the intent expressed in the linked issue or PR description, and where should a human design-owner intervene *before* deeper review is worthwhile. Tags code owners on findings that require human design adjudication. Keywords: F Prime design, FPP, topology, architectural fit, design intent, reuse, code-owner adjudication. ([nasa/fprime](https://github.com/nasa/fprime/blob/HEAD/.github/agents/design-review.agent.md) · ★12k)
- **F Prime Development Agent**: Use when developing F Prime flight software: creating or extending components, wiring or evolving deployment topologies, or making build-system (CMake) changes. Sequences the development skills in the correct phase order — requirements, design, implementation, unit test, integration test for components; implementation then integration test for topology deltas. Keywords: F Prime, development, component, topology, deployment, CMake, FPP, lifecycle, flight software. ([nasa/fprime](https://github.com/nasa/fprime/blob/HEAD/.github/agents/fprime-development.agent.md) · ★12k)
- **F Prime Full-Codebase Review Orchestrator**: Entry point for the F Prime full-codebase (batch) review. Partitions the entire repository into review units, drives every registered reviewer over every unit reading files directly from the working tree, and writes diffable finding artifacts plus a resumable ledger to a results repository. Use this when you want the whole codebase reviewed exhaustively, not a PR. ([nasa/fprime](https://github.com/nasa/fprime/blob/HEAD/.github/agents/full-codebase-review.agent.md) · ★12k)
- **F Prime Maintainability & Readability Reviewer**: Use when reviewing F Prime PRs for maintainability and readability: unclear or misleading naming, oversized / overly-complex functions, deep nesting and convoluted control flow, copy-paste duplication in production code, dead or commented-out code introduced by the diff, misleading or stale inline comments, unclear parameter shapes (boolean-flag arguments, long positional lists), inconsistency with the surrounding file's local conventions, inexplicit return values (bare bools / int status codes), overly-clever constructs, and fragile code structure whose shape silently carries behavior (asymm… ([nasa/fprime](https://github.com/nasa/fprime/blob/HEAD/.github/agents/maintainability-review.agent.md) · ★12k)
- **F Prime Operational Consequences Reviewer**: Use when reviewing F Prime PRs for operational consequences: what happens on a flight system when documented behavior occurs at the worst possible time. Assumes the code is locally correct (other reviewers check that) and reviews as the operator who must fly the system. - **F Prime PR Review Orchestrator**: Entry point for the F Prime multi-agent PR review. - **F Prime System Test Builder**: Use when an approved F Prime system test plan must be turned into a runnable harness (stage 2 of the system test process): tester components, integration tests, bench scripts, record markers, `@planted_fault` fault-injection declarations and `@helper` predicates for the fth engine, iterated with the system-test reviewer through mutation vectors and a stage-3 rehearsal. - **F Prime System Test Designer**: Use when a human asks for a system-level or hardware-in-the-loop test plan for an F Prime component, driver, or subsystem (stage 2 of the system test process, or the test-design phase of component development). Reads the goals, requirements, and SDD, and produces a requirements-traced plan with a machine-evaluable pass rule per test, metrics, and a fault-injection menu, then iterates with the system-test reviewer. - **F Prime System Test Reviewer**: Use when an F Prime system test plan or test harness needs an adversarial review (stage 2 of the system test process, or the test-design phase of component development): finds what the plan would not catch, what the harness would pass by construction, and checks the test functions' named checks against the plan text and the vector records (the auditor replays those same functions). - **F Prime Test Auditor**: Use when auditing a run-for-record of an F Prime system test (stage 3 of the system test process): runs `fth audit` — manifest, record-hash, run-start-marker and flash-provenance checks, re-export, record-integrity findings, replay of the same test functions over the record diffed per test against the runner's hashed junit (DIVERGENCE on any difference; legacy plans: engine plus the reviewer's independent checker), program-rendered quick-look pages — then performs the independent final-state check and reports divergences, blind to the runner's verdicts and to the seal. ([debabsah/fable-method](https://github.com/debabsah/fable-method/blob/HEAD/agents/fable-lens.md) · ★1)
- **fable-low**: Internal Kerd routing agent for Fable at low reasoning effort. Invoke only when Kerd Conductor or Agent explicitly selects kerd:fable-low for a delegated job; do not choose it for ordinary requests. The name carries the model and the effort so the running-job list shows both. The dispatching call still names the model (fable); this definition sets the same model, so a call that omits it still resolves to Fable, unless the host forces or restricts the subagent model. ([anthonymaley/Kerd](https://github.com/anthonymaley/Kerd/blob/HEAD/agents/fable-low.md) · ★3)

- **fable-low**: General-purpose worker running Fable 5 at low reasoning effort. Use for subtasks that need frontier-model judgment with fast turnaround — non-trivial code changes with a clear spec, careful summarization or review of large material, tricky-but-bounded fixes. Fable at low effort still outperforms prior models at high effort. Prefer opus-low for purely mechanical or routine work (Fable costs more per token). ([Sartonio/ai-first-starter](https://github.com/Sartonio/ai-first-starter/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/fable-low.md))
- **fable-max**: Internal Kerd routing agent for Fable at max reasoning effort. The name carries the model and the effort so the running-job list shows both. The dispatching call still names the model (fable); this definition sets the same model, so a call that omits it still resolves to Fable, unless the host forces or restricts the subagent model. Delegate the hardest, most open-ended, ambiguous, cross-cutting, or multi-step tasks here — deep research, architecture and design, end-to-end implementation, debugging, refactoring, data work, writing, analysis, and anything that needs the strongest model with the deepest reasoning. - **fable-planner**: Fable planner for the /fable skill. Invoked once by /fable with a plan brief — intent, task verbatim, session constraints, file map, pasted load-bearing code, and 1–3 open questions — and returns the plan: numbered steps with risks, checkpoints, assumptions. Resumed at task end for the warm diff-review (SHIP/FIX-THEN-SHIP/RECONSULT), and mid-task only if one of its own checkpoints or assumptions fails. Also accepts a critique brief (driver's DRAFT PLAN included, on explicit user request) and a cold REVIEW-ONLY brief (diff + task spec, no plan engagement) for pre-approved mechanical items. - **factory-architect**: The frontier scalpel. Use ONLY for genuinely novel system architecture, unknown-shape problems, the hardest long-horizon TDD, or multi-repo refactors where being wrong costs major rework — and only after Opus has failed twice or the task is explicitly flagged hardest-reasoning. ([Radix-Obsidian/apex-factory-core](https://github.com/Radix-Obsidian/apex-factory-core/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/factory-architect.md))

([dalsoop-archived/ax-hackerton-2026-mono](https://github.com/dalsoop-archived/ax-hackerton-2026-mono/blob/HEAD/apps/agent-factory/.claude/agents/factory-auditor.md) · ★4 · 2 copies)

- **factory-ci-doctor**: Diagnostician for one red GitHub Actions run. - **factory-critic**: Adversarial reviewer for factory PRs and specs. Argues the case against a change - what breaks, what was assumed, what maintenance cost is being deferred. The vendored LAW-13 engine (factory-code-review) is this lens's instrument. Runs on a different model family than the writer. ([e2its/myrmion-AI-factory](https://github.com/e2its/myrmion-AI-factory/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/factory-critic-fidelity.md) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **factory-critic-governance**: Read-only critic — the governance lens on a completed diff: the bound rules and laws, protected paths, contract-first, extension strategy, the defect catalog. On a governance surface informational is the default and escalation must be justified. ([e2its/myrmion-AI-factory](https://github.com/e2its/myrmion-AI-factory/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/factory-critic-governance.md) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **factory-critic-security**: Read-only critic — the security lens on a completed diff: the SAST pattern library, secrets, injection, authz, data exposure (any credential or personal datum is a path to the product). Do not select for architectural or trivially mechanical work. ([jchandra74/claude-factory](https://github.com/jchandra74/claude-factory/blob/HEAD/agents/factory-implementer.md) · ★1)

([jchandra74/claude-factory](https://github.com/jchandra74/claude-factory/blob/HEAD/agents/factory-implementer-heavy.md) · ★1)

([jchandra74/claude-factory](https://github.com/jchandra74/claude-factory/blob/HEAD/agents/factory-implementer-light.md) · ★1)

The /factory improve command spawns one per repo and base branch to apply the improvements a human accepted on the dashboard, one commit per improvement, in its own worktree. It never pushes. ([yoieh/factory](https://github.com/yoieh/factory/blob/HEAD/agents/factory-improver.md) · 2 copies)
- **factory-infra-scout**: Read-only investigator for deployed infrastructure — Dokploy stacks, servers, containers, databases, DNS, health endpoints. Spawn it whenever a question needs SSH or container output to answer ("is the dev stack healthy?", "what is env var X on the deployed app?", "did the deploy pick up the new image?", "why is smoke red?") so the raw logs and shell output stay out of the caller's context. ([watt-mind/factory](https://github.com/watt-mind/factory/blob/HEAD/plugins/core/agents/factory-infra-scout.md) · ★20 · 6 copies)

- **factory-intent-reviewer**: Intent reviewer of the /factory skill. Checks a finished claude-code-tokscope diff against the owner's verbatim request. Plan-blind and read-only. The /factory land command spawns one per open PR of a done run, to judge the whole PR before it is marked ready or merged: the full test suite, the checks, the diff against its base and every open point. - **factory-merge-reviewer**: Cold, read-only reviewer for one pull request.
